## Current security engineering checkpoint - Local key-file ACL hardening - 2026-11-03

- On Windows, newly created encrypted key backups and temporary files used to restore encryption-key variables now receive an explicit ACL for the current account, SYSTEM and local Administrators before any key bytes are written. ACL failure removes the newly created file and aborts. Non-Windows platforms retain the existing restrictive creation mode.

## Latest verified checkpoint Ã¢â‚¬â€ recover pending approvals when the frozen reviewer set is unavailable Ã¢â‚¬â€ 2026-10-31

- Added a task-reviewer recovery action for pending task-completion approvals only when the immutable submission snapshot has no active, organization/centre-scoped `tasks.approve` member. Reassignment requires both `tasks.assign` and `tasks.approve`, an active target with scoped approval permission, actor/target separation from creator and assignee, reason, version check and idempotency key. The original submission snapshot remains unchanged; the action appends an encrypted `APPROVER_REASSIGNED` event and audit record. Empty named-reviewer snapshots mean role-based approval (any currently authorized reviewer) and are never offered for replacement. The API also rejects recovery while any snapshot reviewer remains eligible. The UI exposes candidates only when a nonempty named snapshot has no active reviewer, and history resolves the reviewer label without returning the raw staff ID.
- Added migration **0078** to allow the append-only event type. It was applied to the local development database; `db:integration` replayed **78 migrations** and passed runtime-role/append-only checks. PostgreSQL integration verifies stale-reviewer reassignment, encrypted event/history readback, denial when reassignment is unnecessary, then successful approval by the effective reviewer.
- Verification: task route tests **16/16**; focused PostgreSQL integration **1/1** (26 unrelated cases skipped); full default suite **411/411 across 95 files**; lint, TypeScript, production build (**208 generated routes/pages**) and dependency audit (**0 vulnerabilities**) passed.

## Latest verified checkpoint ???????? approval escalation persistence fix ???????? 2026-10-30

## Screen inventory reconciliation

`SCREEN_SPEC_178.md` defines 178 target screens, but it is a proposal/specification, not a completion ledger. Route inventory from the latest production build includes 164 generated paths (API routes included). Existing staff/public/admin experiences cover only a subset. The route count must not be compared as if 158 of 178 screens passed acceptance: many routes are APIs or nested/dynamic variants, and several target experiences (patient portal, doctor/corporate/partner, communications/AI and marketing) have no functional implementation.

A screen-by-screen status should be filled from the IDs in `SCREEN_SPEC_178.md` during Wave 8 reconciliation; do not mark unspecified screens implemented based on route count alone.

## Acceptance gates intentionally deferred to the consolidated campaign

1. Owner and provisioned staff flows against actual local browser sessions, including required MFA.
2. Unauthorized, incomplete-MFA, revoked-session, cross-centre and cross-organization live HTTP denial.
3. Full lead-to-patient-to-appointment-to-order-to-collection-to-receiving/dispatch-to-report workflow, with only evidence-backed external states.
4. Desktop and narrow-mobile layout review across roles.
5. Scanner-backed report lifecycle; current environment lacks a qualified scanner and fails closed.
6. Full encryption-key backup/restore and rotation/recovery evidence; finance/report metadata encryption and populated legacy backfill passed in an isolated synthetic schema.
7. Business and clinical approvals: specimen SOPs, critical-result escalation, invoice issuer/tax/settlement, consent/privacy/retention, backups and disaster recovery.

Do not convert a deferred gate into a pass because an automated unit test or successful build exists.

## 2026-10-30 checkpoint ???????? retry-safe appointment creation

The appointment request form now fingerprints the request payload with the shared IdempotencyAttempt helper. An unchanged retry after a network/5xx uncertainty reuses the key; corrected or otherwise changed booking details receive a new key; a successful or definitive client response clears the attempt. Error copy no longer claims that an ambiguous request definitely did not save. This prevents duplicate bookings and avoids key-reuse conflicts when a staff member edits a failed request. No schema/API migration change.

Verification: focused idempotency and PostgreSQL operations regression **12/12**; full parallel suite **280/280 across 71 files**; standalone typecheck, lint, and production build (**172 generated paths**) passed. ## 2026-10-30 checkpoint ???????? retry-safe reception check-in

Reception check-in now retains an idempotency key per appointment across uncertain network or server responses in both the arrival queue and appointment detail page. A retry of the same arrival request therefore replays the original result instead of generating a second request key. If another attempt has already recorded arrival, the UI refreshes the appointment and reports that arrival is already recorded; it does not imply a duplicate was created. No schema or migration change.

## 2026-10-30 checkpoint - retry-safe roster mutations

- Roster create, update and cancellation requests now preserve an idempotency key across unchanged UI retries after network or server uncertainty. Roster update/cancel keys and request hashes are stored on append-only shift events, scoped to the organization, and protected by a transaction advisory lock. An identical retry returns the original outcome without another shift version, event or audit entry; changed payload reuse receives a conflict. Authentication, centre authorization, timezone checks, overlap detection and optimistic version checks remain enforced.

## 2026-10-30 checkpoint - approval deadline queues prioritize configured deadlines

- Fixed a due-queue ordering defect in `GET /api/tasks`: approval-expiry and approval-escalation queues were filtered by their configured deadlines but sorted by the optional generic task due date. PostgreSQL places null values first for ascending sorts, so a paginated manager queue could place tasks without a generic due date ahead of the earliest-expiring approvals. Each queue now sorts by its own configured deadline with explicit `NULLS LAST`; other task views keep generic due date ordering with nulls last.
- Added a disposable PostgreSQL API regression with 101 synthetic due approvals whose generic due date is null. Both approval-expiry and escalation-due pagination return the first 100 in true deadline order and report a further page. The fixture uses an isolated synthetic centre to prevent queue/dashboard contamination.
- Verification: appointment/task PostgreSQL route integration **19/19**; default parallel suite **334/334 across 80 files**; TypeScript, lint, production build (**186 generated paths**), and `db:integration` (**63 migrations replayed**, runtime-role/RBAC/append-only checks) passed. Live browser/mobile acceptance, remaining security controls and production qualification remain open. Synthetic data only.

## 2026-10-30 checkpoint - roster conflicts include authorized peers across pages

- Fixed a pagination boundary gap in the roster conflict API. Previously, overlap flags were computed only from the appointments and collection jobs on the current page; an overlapping pair split across page 1/page 2 could be invisible on both pages. The API now checks each current-page item against same-organization, same-centre appointments and collection jobs in the overlapping time window, then returns only the visible item's conflict flag. Peer details are not selected into the response. The overlap checks preserve each source API's broad-versus-assigned-only scope.
- Added a PostgreSQL regression with 101 synthetic appointments, placing a conflicting pair on opposite sides of the 100-row page boundary. Both records now receive `OVERLAPPING_ASSIGNMENT` on their respective pages. The test uses an isolated centre and encrypts appointment private data with the normal application helper.

### 2026-10-31 ??? Retry-safe doctor relationship workflows

Doctor profile creation, profile edits and referral activity now use stable idempotency attempts. If a response is lost, retrying unchanged values reuses the same key; changed values produce a distinct key, and definitive client errors clear the attempt. The referral form captures its DOM element before awaiting the request, so a confirmed save can reliably reset the form. User-facing status text now distinguishes an unconfirmed response and directs staff to retry unchanged details safely. No schema or migration change.

Verification: full default `npm test` **358/358 across 84 files**; whole-project lint and TypeScript passed; production build passed (**197 generated pages/routes**). ### 2026-10-31 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Reassign pending approval escalations before deadline

Closed an operational dead-end where the designated escalation approver could become unavailable, but managers could reassign only after the deadline. A pending task-completion escalation can now be reassigned before or after its current deadline by a staff member with both `tasks.assign` and `tasks.approve` in the centre. The API still requires the new owner to be an active, in-scope approver present in the immutable submission snapshot, distinct from the task creator and assignee, and requires a future due time, reason, current version and idempotency key. The transactional update preserves the pending approval and appends an encrypted `ESCALATION_REASSIGNED` event plus audit event; it does not approve or reject the task. The workbench now exposes the action while an approval is pending.

Added a route regression for an early reassignment and retained the rejection test for selecting the creator/assignee. Focused task route/policy tests **22/22**; full default suite **401/401 across 93 files**; whole-project lint and TypeScript passed; production build passed (**208 generated pages/routes**). Synthetic data only.

Named policies snapshot only currently eligible named staff, reject when the set has no eligible approver, and reject a configured escalation owner who is no longer eligible. ## 2026-10-31 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Fail-closed ClamAV reply parsing

Tightened the local ClamAV INSTREAM response parser. A document is classified CLEAN only for one complete NUL-terminated `stream: OK` record; FOUND, ERROR (including text containing Ã¢â‚¬Å“OKÃ¢â‚¬Â), malformed, multiple, truncated and oversized replies cannot clear quarantine. Scanner endpoints are restricted to explicit loopback names/addresses because the ClamAV TCP protocol has no transport authentication or encryption. Regression tests cover these reply cases and reject remote endpoints. Focused report-storage suite passed 4/4; full default suite passed 425/425 across 98 files on the final source state; lint and TypeScript passed. No schema, migration, dependency or persistent-data change. A local ClamAV service is not installed/configured, so no real clean or malware scan was claimed; report documents remain blocked in quarantine without scanner clearance. Key recovery/rotation qualification, live authorization, browser/mobile acceptance and production qualification remain open. Synthetic data only.

## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Enforce support/task visibility in the SQL queue

## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Roster and workload APIs enforce self scope for basic readers

Closed a staff privacy gap in `/api/roster` and `/api/roster/workload`: a user with only `roster.read` could retrieve other staff members' private shift notes and team-wide names/workload. Basic roster readers are now limited in SQL to their own shifts and metrics; requesting another worker's shift returns `not_found`. ## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Prevent invalid approval escalations from starving due work

Fixed a fairness defect in the approval deadline worker. Previously, an overdue escalation whose owner became inactive, lost `tasks.approve`, moved out of scope, or conflicted with the task creator/assignee could be selected repeatedly and skipped in application code. More than one full batch of such rows could starve valid approvals and escalations indefinitely. The worker now prefilters eligible escalation owners in SQL before selecting its bounded batch, separately reports the count of stale/conflicting due assignments, and leaves those tasks pending and repairable by an authorized manager. Expiration still takes precedence and can proceed even if an escalation owner is invalid. The worker never approves or rejects a task automatically.

A PostgreSQL regression seeds 55 invalid overdue assignments (more than the worker batch size), a valid due escalation, and a due expiry. It verifies the valid work is processed, all 55 stale items remain pending without fabricated events, and the worker reports the skipped count. Focused disposable PostgreSQL route integration: **29/29**. Full default suite: **468/468 across 109 files**. `npm run db:integration` passed with **79 migrations** and runtime-role checks; TypeScript, whole-project lint, and production build (**211 generated paths**) passed. No migration, dependency, persistent business data, Owner account, key, or audit-history change. Patient accounts remain disabled pending approved identity proofing; live authenticated authorization, browser/mobile review, scanner/key lifecycle qualification, and production readiness remain open. Synthetic data only.

### Shift handover replay rechecks record authorization Ã¢â‚¬â€ 2026-11-01

A same-centre staff member with handover permission could previously replay an existing matching key and receive a handover ID without being a participant or roster manager. Replay now verifies the caller is the stored outgoing/incoming participant or has centre-scoped `roster.manage`; unrelated callers receive a non-disclosing 403. ## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Public sitemap and crawler boundaries

Added `src/app/sitemap.ts` and `src/app/robots.ts` for the public website. The sitemap contains the approved public static pages and obtains dynamic centre/service URLs only from the existing public catalogue read model, which already filters to active, explicitly published, verified, effective catalogue versions. It deduplicates and bounds output below the sitemap URL limit, and retains static public routes if catalogue lookup is unavailable. Crawler rules disallow account, staff, patient and operational paths; this is an indexing directive only and does not replace the existing authentication/authorization controls. Production sitemap/robots metadata is emitted only when an explicit HTTPS `PUBLIC_SITE_URL` or `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is configured; local development uses `BETTER_AUTH_URL` or localhost. `.env.example` documents the optional canonical origin.

Focused tests passed **7/7 across 2 files**, covering canonical-origin validation, production fail-closed behavior, public-only paths, published catalogue URL inclusion, catalogue outage fallback and crawler exclusions. No schema, migration, persistent database, Owner account, encryption key or external service changed. The full suite/typecheck/lint/build are pending for this code change. The public website and Centre & Staff Operations remain incomplete; patient accounts remain disabled pending identity-proofing approval. Synthetic data only.

### Public crawler metadata verification addendum Ã¢â‚¬â€ 2026-11-01

The route also caps output below 50,000 URLs and requires a configured HTTPS canonical origin in production.

### Escalation idempotent replay rechecks action authority Ã¢â‚¬â€ 2026-11-01

The escalation PATCH route previously looked up and returned an idempotent event result before checking whether the current caller was allowed to perform that action. A centre-wide read-only user who knew a valid idempotency key could receive the result of a privileged action. The route now rechecks the centre-scoped action permission (or the assigned-owner rule for acknowledge/comment/resolve) before reading or decrypting a replay result. Existing idempotent behavior remains for authorized callers and does not repeat the mutation.

A regression was first run against the old behavior and reproduced the defect (it returned the cached response instead of 403); the fixed route now denies the read-only replay before event lookup/decryption. A complementary test confirms an authorized manager receives the original result without another update. Focused escalation-detail tests **4/4**; full default suite **494/494 across 116 files**; TypeScript, lint, and production build (**213 routes/pages**) passed. ## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Communication draft cancellation lifecycle

Added a permission-protected same-origin `PATCH` action to discard an existing unsent communication draft. The route rechecks staff MFA/RBAC and organization/centre scope, locks the lead and draft transactionally, uses `updatedAt` optimistic concurrency, and appends a `CANCELLED` event plus a content-free audit event. ## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Public enquiry contact submission quota

The unauthenticated public enquiry write path now limits matching phone/email contacts to five submissions per rolling 24-hour window across the organization. Quota checks use the existing purpose-specific HMAC blind indexes, not plaintext contact values. PostgreSQL advisory locks serialize concurrent submissions on each sorted contact key; the idempotency replay check runs first so an unchanged retry returns the original accepted response without consuming quota again. The form gives a plain 429 wait-and-retry message. No external anti-bot provider, proxy header, IP address, patient account, or messaging integration is used.

Verification: dedicated populated PostgreSQL API integration **9/9**, including six concurrent same-contact requests (five accepted, one rate-limited), unchanged idempotent replay after the quota is reached, and a database count of five stored records; full default suite **525/525 across 124 files**; lint, TypeScript and production build (**215 routes/pages**) passed. No schema migration or persistent development-data change. This is contact-based abuse control, not global/IP rate limiting or complete bot defense. Public legal/privacy copy, live browser acceptance, external integrations, security gates and production qualification remain open. Synthetic data only.

## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Internal communication template management

Added a centre-scoped template library in the Communications workspace. Staff with `leads.followups.manage` can create and edit encrypted draft templates, activate them for internal use, archive them, and inspect version/decision history. Templates accept a small allowlist of merge variables and reject malformed or unsupported tokens. Template content and event snapshots use the existing AES-GCM operational encryption with organization, centre, record and field context. Changes are version-checked, idempotent, transactional and audited without content in audit details. PostgreSQL event history is append-only; the application role has read/create/update on templates but no delete, and append-only access for history.

Sending, scheduling, preview to a recipient, external providers and delivery claims remain disabled. Activating a template is an internal status only and does not establish patient contact consent or marketing consent. Patient portal accounts remain disabled pending approved identity proofing. Synthetic data only.

## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Private-data-safe idempotency fingerprints

## 2026-11-01 Ã¢â‚¬â€ Patient/contact field-key rotation qualification

Added a restartable key-rotation utility for `patient_contact_secrets`: phone, optional email, profile and name envelopes are decrypted with the row's existing key ID, rebound to the configured active field key, re-encrypted with fresh AES-GCM nonces, and updated under a row lock with compare-and-swap checks. Blind-index values are deliberately preserved; this work does not rotate the separate blind-index key. A local-development command requires an explicit typed confirmation and advises coordinated database/key backup. It was not run against the persistent development database, and no configured key was changed.

The disposable populated synthetic-data qualification now tests a missing historical key, tampered ciphertext, one-row committed interruption, resume, repeat-run idempotency, exact decrypted phone/email/profile/name parity, blind-index preservation, and a full 89-migration replay twice. ## 2026-11-02 checkpoint - Escalation comments preserve immutable event history

Escalation comments now persist only in their encrypted `COMMENTED` event and no longer append into or rewrite the escalation's original encrypted description. The action still increments the escalation version transactionally, and the API history renders the comment from its event. This avoids description mutation, duplicate storage, and truncation of the original description while preserving idempotency and authorization. Added a disposable PostgreSQL integration assertion for unchanged ciphertext plaintext, event history retrieval, and queue projection identifier minimization. No schema migration or dependency change.

Verification: focused escalation PostgreSQL integration **4/4**; full default suite **624/624 across 155 files**; TypeScript, lint, and production build passed (**231 generated page entries**, two workers). No persistent development data, Owner account, key, or audit history was changed. This closes an escalation-history correctness defect only; sensitive domain approval types remain disabled without approved policy. Centre & Staff Operations and the full development backlog remain active. Patient accounts remain disabled pending identity-proofing approval; browser/mobile and live authorization acceptance, key recovery/rotation/restore, malware scanning and production qualification remain open. Synthetic data only.

### Verification correction - integration pool cleanup and full-suite recovery (2026-11-02)

The prior full-suite failures were caused by integration fixtures leaving the process-global Drizzle/PostgreSQL pool bound to disposable schemas, and by cleanup hooks using the short default budget while dropping fully migrated schemas. Added `closeDatabasePool()` to close and clear the cached client pool, applied it to the schema-based PostgreSQL fixtures, and restored `DATABASE_URL` to the dedicated test database after cleanup. Three short API tests also import route modules outside their timed assertions. The roster handover cleanup uses a bounded 60-second teardown hook. No application migration, dependency, production DB, account, key, or business record changed.
